}; elif [ -f "${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}" ]; then GSD_TOOLS="${ANTIGRAVITY_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.gemini/antigravity}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}"; gsd_run() { node "$GSD_TOOLS" "$@"; }; elif [ -f "${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}" ]; then GSD_TOOLS="${OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/opencode}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}"; gsd_run() { node "$GSD_TOOLS" "$@"; }; elif [ -f "${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}" ]; then GSD_TOOLS="${KILO_CONFIG_DIR:-${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/kilo}/gsd-core/bin/${_GSD_SHIM_NAME}"; gsd_run() { node "$GSD_TOOLS" "$@"; }; else echo "ERROR: gsd-tools.cjs not found at $GSD_TOOLS and gsd-tools is not on PATH. Run: npx -y @opengsd/gsd-core@latest --claude --local" >&2; exit 1; fi
12	# Resolve build command: project config > Xcode > Makefile > language sniff
13	BUILD_CMD=$(gsd_run query config-get workflow.build_command --default "" 2>/dev/null || true)
14	if [ -z "$BUILD_CMD" ]; then
15	  XCODEPROJ=$(find . -maxdepth 2 -name "*.xcodeproj" -not -path "*/node_modules/*" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
16	  if [ -n "$XCODEPROJ" ]; then
17	    # Xcode project: get first scheme from xcodebuild -list -json
18	    XCODE_SCHEME=$(xcodebuild -list -json -project "$XCODEPROJ" 2>/dev/null | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d.get('project',{}).get('schemes',[None])[0] or '')" 2>/dev/null || true)
19	    if [ -n "$XCODE_SCHEME" ]; then
20	      BUILD_CMD="xcodebuild build -scheme '$XCODE_SCHEME' -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 16'"
21	    else
22	      BUILD_CMD="xcodebuild build -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 16'"
23	    fi
24	  elif [ -f "Makefile" ] && grep -q "^build:" Makefile; then
25	    BUILD_CMD="make build"
26	  elif [ -f "Justfile" ] || [ -f "justfile" ]; then
27	    BUILD_CMD="just build"
28	  elif [ -f "Cargo.toml" ]; then
29	    BUILD_CMD="cargo build"
30	  elif [ -f "go.mod" ]; then
31	    BUILD_CMD="go build ./..."
32	  elif [ -f "pyproject.toml" ] || [ -f "requirements.txt" ]; then
33	    BUILD_CMD="python -m py_compile $(find . -name '*.py' -not -path './.planning/*' -not -path './node_modules/*' | head -20 | tr '\n' ' ')"
34	  elif [ -f "package.json" ] && grep -q '"build"' package.json; then
35	    BUILD_CMD="npm run build"
36	  else
37	    BUILD_CMD=""
38	    echo "⚠ No build command detected — skipping build gate"
39	  fi
40	fi
41	# Run build with 5-minute timeout
42	BUILD_EXIT=0
43	if [ -n "$BUILD_CMD" ]; then
44	  timeout 300 bash -c "$BUILD_CMD" 2>&1
45	  BUILD_EXIT=$?
46	  if [ "${BUILD_EXIT}" -eq 0 ]; then
47	    echo "✓ Post-merge build gate passed"
48	  elif [ "${BUILD_EXIT}" -eq 124 ]; then
49	    echo "⚠ Post-merge build gate timed out after 5 minutes"
50	  else
51	    echo "✗ Post-merge build gate failed (exit code ${BUILD_EXIT})"
52	    WAVE_FAILURE_COUNT=$((WAVE_FAILURE_COUNT + 1))
53	  fi
54	fi
55	```
56	
57	**If `BUILD_EXIT` is 0 (pass):** `✓ Build gate passed` → proceed to Test gate.
58	
59	**If `BUILD_EXIT` is 124 (timeout):** Log warning, treat as non-blocking, continue to Test gate.
60	
61	**If `BUILD_EXIT` is non-zero (build failure):** Increment `WAVE_FAILURE_COUNT` (same semantics as test failures). Present failure output and offer "Fix now" or "Continue" options (same as step 5.8).
62	
63	**Step B — Test gate:**
64	
65	```bash
66	# Resolve test command: project config > Xcode > Makefile > language sniff
67	TEST_CMD=$(gsd_run query config-get workflow.test_command --default "" 2>/dev/null || true)
68	if [ -z "$TEST_CMD" ]; then
69	  XCODEPROJ=$(find . -maxdepth 2 -name "*.xcodeproj" -not -path "*/node_modules/*" 2>/dev/null | head -1)
70	  if [ -n "$XCODEPROJ" ]; then
71	    # Xcode project: reuse scheme detected above (or re-detect)
72	    if [ -z "${XCODE_SCHEME:-}" ]; then
73	      XCODE_SCHEME=$(xcodebuild -list -json -project "$XCODEPROJ" 2>/dev/null | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d.get('project',{}).get('schemes',[None])[0] or '')" 2>/dev/null || true)
74	    fi
75	    if [ -n "$XCODE_SCHEME" ]; then
76	      TEST_CMD="xcodebuild test -scheme '$XCODE_SCHEME' -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 16'"
77	    else
78	      TEST_CMD="xcodebuild test -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 16'"
79	    fi
80	  elif [ -f "Makefile" ] && grep -q "^test:" Makefile; then
81	    TEST_CMD="make test"
82	  elif [ -f "Justfile" ] || [ -f "justfile" ]; then
83	    TEST_CMD="just test"
84	  elif [ -f "package.json" ]; then
85	    TEST_CMD="npm test"
86	  elif [ -f "Cargo.toml" ]; then
87	    TEST_CMD="cargo test"
88	  elif [ -f "go.mod" ]; then
89	    TEST_CMD="go test ./..."
90	  elif [ -f "pyproject.toml" ] || [ -f "requirements.txt" ]; then
91	    TEST_CMD="python -m pytest -x -q --tb=short 2>&1 || uv run python -m pytest -x -q --tb=short"
92	  else
93	    TEST_CMD="true"
94	    echo "⚠ No test runner detected — skipping post-merge test gate"
95	  fi
96	fi
97	# Run test suite with 5-minute timeout
98	TEST_EXIT=0
99	timeout 300 bash -c "$TEST_CMD" 2>&1
100	TEST_EXIT=$?
101	if [ "${TEST_EXIT}" -eq 0 ]; then
102	  echo "✓ Post-merge test gate passed — no cross-plan conflicts"
103	elif [ "${TEST_EXIT}" -eq 124 ]; then
104	  echo "⚠ Post-merge test gate timed out after 5 minutes"
105	else
106	  echo "✗ Post-merge test gate failed (exit code ${TEST_EXIT})"
107	  WAVE_FAILURE_COUNT=$((WAVE_FAILURE_COUNT + 1))
108	fi
109	```
110	
111	**If `TEST_EXIT` is 0 (pass):** `✓ Post-merge test gate: {N} tests passed — no cross-plan conflicts` → continue to orchestrator tracking update.
112	
113	**If `TEST_EXIT` is 124 (timeout):** Log warning, treat as non-blocking, continue. Tests may need a longer budget or manual run.
114	
115	**If `TEST_EXIT` is non-zero (test failure):** Increment `WAVE_FAILURE_COUNT` to track
116	cumulative failures across waves. Subsequent waves should report:
117	`⚠ Note: ${WAVE_FAILURE_COUNT} prior wave(s) had test failures`
118	
```

Review written to `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/phases/50-ipfs-ipns-data-integrity-fixes/50-REVIEW.md`.

## Findings: 2 BLOCKER, 5 WARNING, 3 INFO

**Critical:**

- **CR-01** — `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` (`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:272-325`) drops the `visited`/cycle guard that the sibling `ensureFolderLoaded` DFS carries (client.ts:549). Folder metadata is decrypted, attacker/corruption-influenceable data; a folder cycle (A→B→A) recurses unbounded → stack overflow. The per-child catch at line 317 does not break it because the recursive call swallows its own errors and resolves rather than throwing.
- **CR-02** — All four fire-and-forget dispatch sites (client.ts:938, 1950, 1976, 2013) chain only `.then()` with **no `.catch()`**. The collector can reject (CR-01's overflow, or a top-level throw in the `Promise.all` collect path), producing an unhandled promise rejection — directly contradicting the "failures never block the caller" docstring contract.

**Warnings:**

- **WR-01** — Drain worker's refcount re-check (`pending-unpin.processor.ts:59-70`) runs the `count` + physical `unpinFile` **outside any advisory lock**, unlike `guardedUnpin` which takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` precisely to serialize this. A concurrent `recordPin` for the same deduped CID lands between read and unpin → live pin removed → Kubo GC → data loss. Wider window than the D-13 note acknowledges (up to 5 min in outbox).
- **WR-02** — CID regex inconsistent: `UnpinDto` uses `Qm...{44}` + `@MaxLength(255)`; `RegisterCidDto` uses open-ended `{44,}` and **no MaxLength** — same logical value, divergent validation, missing the DoS bound.
- **WR-03** — `guardedUnpin` post-commit `delete({ cid })` (vault.service.ts:317) runs after the advisory lock is released; a racing path can delete the outbox retry row the other path depends on.
- **WR-04** — `deleteItem` traversal fan-out is unbounded (no `p-limit`, unlike upload path); request storm on large subtrees, compounded by CR-01.
- **WR-05** — `LocalProvider.unpinFile` interpolates CID into `pin/rm?arg=${cid}` unescaped; latent for DB-sourced CIDs that flow through the new drain/guarded paths.

**Info:** IN-01 (triplicated `IPFS_PROVIDER` factory), IN-02 (duplicate-name accumulation, fixed by CR-01's set), IN-03 (BYO rows shadow drift orphan detection).

The well-tested wins (advisory-lock INT_MIN fix, in-transaction quota decrement, drain refcount-skip, backfill empty/unreachable-Kubo guards) check out. The on-demand traversal is where this phase regressed against its own `ensureFolderLoaded` precedent — CR-01 and CR-02 are the must-fix pair.

All findings handled. Summary below.

## Fixed (in-scope)

| Finding | Commit | What changed |
|---|---|---|
| **CR-01** + **CR-02** + **WR-04** | `d70714412` | `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` |
| **WR-01** | `5a42a36f5` | `apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts` |
| **WR-03** | `7ecbf5a33` | `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts` |

- **CR-01** — Added a `visited: Set<string>` to `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync`, seeded on entry, with a skip-before-recurse check (mirrors `ensureFolderLoaded` ~line 549). Threaded through the recursive call; the public callers (~220/246) keep their two-arg signatures since `visited` defaults. Added a regression test (Test D) proving an A→B→A cycle terminates and each ipnsName is collected exactly once.
- **CR-02** — Added `.catch()` logging via `console.warn` (the existing logger in this file) to all four fire-and-forget dispatch sites (~938, ~1962, ~1989, ~2013). Fire-and-forget semantics unchanged.
- **WR-04** — Bounded the per-entry subtree fan-out in `emptyBin`/`purge` with `pLimit(UNENROLL_COLLECT_CONCURRENCY=8)`. (The in-subtree recursion was already sequential via `await`; the unbounded fan-out was the `Promise.all(...map(...))` across bin entries.)
- **WR-01** — Wrapped each drain row's recheck + unpin + outbox-delete in a `dataSource.transaction` that first takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(cid)::bigint)` (same lock as `guardedUnpin`). Injected `DataSource`. Regression asserts lock → refcount-check → unpin ordering. **Logic-bug class** — worth a human sanity-check that holding the advisory lock across the Kubo network call in the drain is acceptable (low-frequency batched path; noted in the code comment).
- **WR-03** — Moved the post-commit outbox-row `delete({ cid })` into a short post-commit transaction that re-takes the same advisory lock, using the manager-scoped `PendingUnpin` repo. Kubo unpin stays outside the lock (D-03 ordering). Removed the now-unused injected `pendingUnpinRepository` field. Spec updated to assert lock-around-delete.

## Deferred (out of phase scope) — `00799c2f8`

- **WR-02** → `.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-19-register-cid-dto-validation-inconsistency.md`
- **WR-05** → `.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-19-local-provider-unescaped-cid-in-pin-url.md`

IN-01/IN-02/IN-03 skipped (IN-02 de-dup was incidentally resolved by the CR-01 `visited` set).

## Test results (final, all green)

- `@cipherbox/sdk` `collect-subtree-ipns-names` — **4/4 passed**
- `@cipherbox/api` `vault.service.spec` + `pending-unpin.processor.spec` + `ipfs.controller.spec` — **90/90 passed**
- SDK typecheck (`tsc -p tsconfig.build.json --noEmit`) — clean
- API typecheck — clean for all modified files; the only `tsc` errors are 3 pre-existing, unrelated spec failures (`http-metrics.interceptor.spec`, `share-invite.service.spec`, `shares.controller.spec`) in files I did not touch.

Worked on the main working tree as instructed; all hooks ran on each commit (no `--no-verify`).

## Verification Complete

**Status:** passed
**Score:** 3/3 must-haves verified
**Report:** `.planning/phases/50-ipfs-ipns-data-integrity-fixes/50-VERIFICATION.md`

All three concrete goal claims hold in code:

**Claim 1 — INT_MIN CID stays deletable**

`apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:267` contains `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($1)::bigint)`. No `abs(hashtext` string appears anywhere in the file. The preceding comment at :264-266 explicitly documents the INT_MIN overflow root cause and cites D-01/WR-01. Regression test at `vault.service.spec.ts:1030` asserts the SQL matches `/pg_advisory_xact_lock/` and does not match `/abs\(hashtext/`.

**Claim 2 — Re-pinned CID is never drained**

`pending-unpin.processor.ts:85-111` wraps each row's recheck, unpin, and outbox delete in a single `dataSource.transaction`. The advisory lock is acquired at :89 before the `count` call at :95. If `refs > 0`, the outbox row is deleted and the method returns without calling `ipfsProvider.unpinFile`. The lock is held across the `unpinFile` call (the noted caveat — deliberate, documented in the code comment at :80-83). WR-03 regression describe block at `pending-unpin.processor.spec.ts:272`.

**Claim 3 — Every descendant IPNS record unenrolled even for unloaded subtrees**

`client.ts:282-348` — `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` has a `visited: Set<string>` parameter (CR-01 fix), checks `visited.has(folderIpnsName)` before recursing (:293), calls `sdkCore.loadFolderMetadata` on a folderTree miss (:303), and never assigns to `this.folderTree` anywhere in the method. All four dispatch sites chain `.catch(err => console.warn(...))` (CR-02 fix at :963, :1975, :2005, :2046). Test D in `collect-subtree-ipns-names.test.ts:288` covers the A→B→A cycle termination case (4 tests total in the file).

**Open caveat (recorded, not blocking):** The drain's advisory lock is held across the Kubo network call — intentional per the in-code comment but creates a lock-duration exposure under a slow/hung Kubo endpoint.

**REQUIREMENTS.md HARD-01 status** remains "Planned" in the tracker — documentation staleness, not a code issue.

════════ 2026-06-18-phase42-unpin-integrity-review-open-findings ════════
---
created: 2026-06-18T00:00:00.000Z
title: Phase 42 unpin-integrity code-review findings (WR/IN) are unresolved in current code
area: bug
severity: high
source: .planning/phases/42-api-unpin-integrity/42-REVIEW.md (no resolution section); re-verified against live code 2026-06-18
files:
  - apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts
  - apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts
  - apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts
  - apps/api/src/ipfs/dto/unpin.dto.ts
  - scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts
---

## Problem

`42-REVIEW.md` raised 7 warnings + 6 info findings on the guarded-unpin / pending-unpins
implementation and has **no resolution section**. Each was re-checked against current code on
2026-06-18 and confirmed still present. Two are correctness risks:

- **WR-01 (high)** — `vault.service.ts:262` `SELECT abs(hashtext($1))::bigint`. For the CID whose
  `hashtext == INT_MIN (-2147483648)`, `abs(int4)` raises `ERROR: integer out of range` —
  deterministically, every unpin of that CID 500s, and its quota row is permanently stuck. The
  file becomes **permanently undeletable via the API**. (`pg_advisory_xact_lock` accepts signed
  bigint, so the `abs()` is both unnecessary and the only failure mode.)
- **WR-03 (high)** — `pending-unpin.processor.ts:53` `drainPendingUnpins` unpins every outbox CID
  unconditionally. If a CID is re-pinned/re-recorded (re-upload of identical ciphertext, or a
  pin-migration flow) while still in `pending_unpins`, the next drain pass removes a **live pin**
  → content eligible for Kubo GC → **data loss**. The D-13 "sub-second window" rationale does not
  cover the outbox path (window ≥ 5 min, unbounded while Kubo is down).

════════ 2026-06-18-unenroll-skips-unloaded-subtrees ════════
---
created: 2026-06-18T00:00:00.000Z
title: collectSubtreeIpnsNames skips unloaded subtrees, leaving nested IPNS records un-unenrolled
area: bug
severity: medium
source: Phase 29 (29-02-SUMMARY key-decisions); verified against live code 2026-06-18
files:
  - packages/sdk/src/client.ts
---

## Problem

Phase 29 wired TEE/IPNS unenrollment into all four deletion paths via
`collectSubtreeIpnsNames`, but the collector only walks **already-loaded** folder state
(`folderTree.get()` and an early return when a folder is not loaded —
`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:232`). Deleting a folder whose subtree was never expanded in the
session returns only the top folder's IPNS name, so **nested file/folder IPNS records below an
unloaded subtree are never unenrolled**. Those records keep being republished by the TEE (wasting
compute) and linger until natural expiry.

## Fix

Resolve and traverse the persisted folder metadata for the subtree being deleted (fetch+decrypt
child folder metadata on demand) rather than relying on the in-memory `folderTree`, so the full set
of descendant IPNS names is collected regardless of load state. Alternatively, pair with the
"periodic unenroll reconciliation job" already in BACKLOG as a backstop.

## Acceptance

Deleting a folder with an unloaded subtree unenrolls every descendant file/folder IPNS name (assert
```

- **#14** — `collectSubtreeIpnsNames` skips unloaded subtrees → fixed by 50-03's on-demand traversal.

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:2000-2007` and `:2041-2048` — The `emptyBin` and `purgeBin` collect-then-dispatch blocks are near-verbatim copy-paste: both create `pLimit(UNENROLL_COLLECT_CONCURRENCY)`, `Promise.all(entries.map(e => collectLimit(() => this.collectBinEntryIpnsNames(e))))`, `.then(nameArrays => this.fireAndForgetUnenroll(nameArrays.flat()))`, `.catch(err => console.warn('[CipherBox] IPNS unenroll collection failed:', err))`. Differs only in the source array (`entriesToUnenroll` vs `purgedEntries`). — Simpler form: extract a private helper `fireAndForgetUnenrollEntries(entries: BinEntry[]): void` that owns the pLimit + Promise.all + flat + catch, and call it from both sites (and `permanentDeleteFromBin` at :1974 collapses into the same helper with a single-entry array). — Concrete cost: ~14 duplicated lines today; any change to the fan-out/error-handling policy must be edited in 2-3 places and risks drift.

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:222-235` and `:248-262` — `collectRemovedItemIpnsNames`'s folder branch and `collectBinEntryIpnsNames`'s folder branch are the same shape: unwrap `folderKeyEncrypted` with the vault private key, call `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync(ipnsName, folderKey)`, and on catch fall back to `[ipnsName]` (push-only difference). — Simpler form: a single private `collectFolderSubtree(ipnsName: string, folderKeyEncrypted: string): Promise<string[]>` that does the unwrap + delegate + catch-fallback; both callers reduce to one line. — Concrete cost: the unwrap+fallback logic is duplicated; if the fallback policy changes it must be updated in two places.

This is a defensible micro-optimization, not dead code — flagging only as a borderline collapse candidate; the inner `:340` check is the load-bearing one. — Simpler form: drop the `:328` early-continue and rely on the function-entry guard (the catch-path `:340` check stays). — Concrete cost: minor — one fewer branch; trade-off is one wasted `unwrapKey` on adversarial cycles. I have enough context. Let me verify one efficiency-relevant detail — whether the within-subtree child loop's sequential `await` per child is by design vs. parallelizable, and check the drift report's `Promise.all` (already concurrent). I have what I need to assess all the named concerns.

Here are my findings from the EFFICIENCY angle.

---

`apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:311-328` — Post-commit Kubo `unpinFile` (network I/O) is awaited, then a **second** advisory-lock transaction is opened solely to `DELETE` the outbox row. This is the WR-03 fix and is correct, but it adds a full round-trip: BEGIN → `pg_advisory_xact_lock` → DELETE → COMMIT after the Kubo call. The lock is *not* held across the network call (good — Kubo is outside), so contention here is low. Concrete cost: one extra DB transaction + advisory-lock acquisition per successful unpin on the hot request path. Cheaper alternative: the row delete doesn't need its own lock acquisition if it used a conditional `DELETE ... WHERE` predicate matching the insert-token, but the lock approach is the cheapest *correct* one given the re-insert-as-retry-safety-net semantics. Net: acceptable, flag only as a minor added round-trip.

`apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts:85-110` (the named concern) — `drainRow` holds `pg_advisory_xact_lock` for the whole transaction, and `ipfsProvider.unpinFile(cid)` (Kubo HTTP call) executes **inside** that transaction (line 107), i.e. the per-CID advisory lock AND an open Postgres transaction/connection are held across network I/O. Assessment: contention is genuinely low here — the lock is per-CID (`hashtext(cid)`), the drain is a low-frequency batched background job, and `BATCH_SIZE` rows are processed **sequentially** (`for` loop at line 54), so at most one Kubo call is in flight at a time. The real cost is **connection-pool occupancy**: a pooled DB connection sits idle-but-open for the full Kubo RTT (plus Kubo's own unpin latency, which can be slow on large pins) once per drained row. Concrete cost: `BATCH_SIZE × KuboUnpinLatency` of held-connection time per drain pass, serialized. Cheaper alternative: take the advisory lock + refcount recheck + outbox delete inside the txn, **commit**, then call `unpinFile` *after* the txn closes (matching `guardedUnpin`'s D-03 ordering) — this releases the DB connection before the network call. The current code chose lock-across-network specifically to prevent a racing `guardedUnpin` re-pin between recheck and unpin; that is a correctness trade-off, so I am not flagging it as a bug — only noting the connection-hold cost is avoidable if the race is instead handled by a post-unpin recheck. Given the batched/low-frequency path, the cost is bounded and acceptable.

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:332-336` — In `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync`, child folders are traversed with `await` **inside a sequential `for...of` loop**, so each subfolder's `unwrapKey` (ECIES decrypt) + recursive `loadFolderMetadata` (IPNS fetch + decrypt) runs strictly one-after-another. For a wide folder (many subfolders at one level), this serializes N independent IPNS fetches. Concrete cost: a folder with K subfolders deep D levels does ~K^D fetches all in series → wall-clock = sum of every fetch RTT. Cheaper alternative: collect the folder children into per-child tasks and run them through a bounded `pLimit` (the `UNENROLL_COLLECT_CONCURRENCY` limiter already imported), as is done one level up for bin entries — i.e. push the concurrency *into* the recursion, not just at the top-level bin-entry fan-out. Caveat: this is fire-and-forget background work off the user's critical path, so latency impact is hidden from the user; flagging as a real but low-severity serialization.

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:297-320` — On-demand traversal **re-fetch assessment** (named concern): within a single collection call, the `visited` set (line 293/328) prevents re-fetching the same IPNS name twice, and in-memory `folderTree` is consulted first (line 298) before any network fetch — so there are **no redundant re-fetches inside one traversal**. However, across the top-level fan-out (`emptyBin`/`purge` at lines 2001-2003 / 2043-2045), each `collectBinEntryIpnsNames` call gets its **own fresh `visited` set**, and each recursion creates new ones — so a CID reachable from two distinct bin entries would be fetched once per entry. In practice bin entries are disjoint subtrees, so this is near-zero waste. No cheaper alternative warranted; the per-call `visited` + folderTree-first short-circuit is already the cheap path. Clean on the re-fetch axis.

`scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts:138-152` — Added `AND pc.pinned_at < NOW() - INTERVAL '1 hour'` and switched projection from `false::boolean` to `v.is_byo_user`. No efficiency concern: still one set-based query, no N+1, and the new predicate is sargable if `pinned_at` is indexed (worth a one-time check, but not introduced-waste). Clean.

`apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts:122` — Upload-rollback now calls `ipfsProvider.unpinFile` directly instead of routing through `guardedUnpin`. This is strictly *less* work (skips the DB ownership/refcount/outbox transaction). Efficiency-positive. Clean.

Net: No correctness flags (out of scope). The two efficiency items worth the author's attention are (1) `drainRow` holding a DB connection + advisory lock across the Kubo network call — bounded and accepted given the batched low-frequency path, and (2) `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` serializing sibling-subfolder IPNS fetches via sequential `await`, which could use the already-imported `pLimit` inside the recursion. Both are off the user's critical path. The named re-fetch concern checks out clean — `visited` + folderTree-first prevents redundant fetches within a traversal.

## ALTITUDE review — Phase 50

Verdict: largely the right depth. Two of your four flagged areas are genuinely well-resolved at the correct altitude; one is correctly accepted debt; one is a real altitude smell worth a small extraction (or at minimum a clearer todo). No correctness bugs flagged per instructions.

---

(a) Per-call-site advisory locking vs. a single `unpinWithLock` primitive — acceptable debt, capture as todo

`apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts:84-110` and `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:259-328`

The same primitive — "take `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(cid)::bigint)` as the first statement, re-check refcount, unpin, delete outbox row" — is now hand-rolled in three places: `guardedUnpin`'s main transaction, `guardedUnpin`'s post-commit row-delete (vault.service.ts:321-324), and `drainRow` (processor). Each copy re-derives the lock-key SQL string and the recheck-then-unpin policy independently.

- Deeper fix: a single owner of the per-CID lock + refcount-recheck policy — e.g. a `withCidLock(cid, fn)` helper and a `refcountAndMaybeUnpin(manager, cid)` on `VaultService` that both the controller-rollback, the post-commit delete, and the drain processor call. The drain's recheck (processor:97-110) is then literally the same code path as `guardedUnpin`'s, not a parallel reimplementation.
- Why it's only debt, not now-worth-fixing: the lock-key SQL is a one-liner and the three copies are currently consistent. The cost is drift risk — the diff itself shows it: the `abs()`/`INT_MIN` fix landed in `guardedUnpin` (vault.service.ts:264-267) but the two new copies were written correctly by hand rather than by sharing the fixed code. The next change to the lock-key derivation (or to "is unpin idempotent enough to hold the lock across the Kubo call") has three sites to find and keep in sync. Concrete cost: a future fix applied to one copy silently diverging from the others, exactly the failure mode this fix just corrected. Capture as a todo: "extract `withCidLock` + shared refcount-recheck so drain/guardedUnpin/rollback share one lock primitive."

(d) Drain refcount-recheck duplicating `guardedUnpin` policy — same root as (a)

`apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts:97-110`

This is the most concrete instance of (a), so I'd fold it into the same todo rather than file it separately. The drain's "count `PinnedCid` under the lock, skip-unpin-but-delete-row if refs>0, else unpin+delete" is the back half of `guardedUnpin` (vault.service.ts:290-307) reimplemented. The policy "BYO advisory rows count toward refcount" (WR-07) is asserted in both places via comments (vault.service.ts:285-289 and processor.ts:124-130) precisely because the count logic is duplicated — if it lived in one `refcountAndMaybeUnpin`, the BYO semantics would be stated and enforced once. Same cost, same fix as (a); not independently worth fixing.

(b) On-demand subtree collector — right altitude, no action

`packages/sdk/src/client.ts:282-348`

This is correctly generalized, not bolted on. `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` mirrors the existing `ensureFolderLoaded` DFS (client.ts:549-619) and the move/copy walk (client.ts:2520-2597): same `loadFolderMetadata` primitive, same `visited`-set cycle guard, same per-child independent try/catch, same "in-memory first, on-demand fetch on miss" shape. It uses the shared `sdkCore.loadFolderMetadata` rather than inventing a new fetch path. The one genuinely distinct constraint — "NEVER write fetched state back to `this.folderTree`" (client.ts:271-276) — is real and well-justified (fire-and-forget timing would re-trigger the documented Zustand/SDK desync). So unenroll-collection reuses folder-metadata traversal as one shared mechanism; it doesn't fork it. No finding.

Minor note (not worth fixing): the visited/acc pattern is now hand-threaded in three traversals (`ensureFolderLoaded`, the move-walk, and this collector) rather than a shared generic `walkSubtree(ipnsName, key, visit)`. That generalization would be a real refactor touching working code with no current bug — out of scope for a hardening phase, and the developer-profile "scope tightly" directive argues against it. Leave it.

(c) IN-04 "accepted circular-dependency" module comments — module boundary is slightly wrong, but acceptable debt

`apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.module.ts:16-20`, `apps/api/src/vault/vault.module.ts:21-27`, `apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.module.ts:20-25`

The comments are honest, but the stated reason ("a shared module would create a circular dependency") is not quite right, and that matters for whether this is the right boundary. `IPFS_PROVIDER` is a leaf: its factory depends only on `ConfigService` (`new LocalProvider(apiUrl, gatewayUrl)`) — it does not depend on `VaultModule` or `IpfsModule` at all. A standalone `IpfsProviderModule { imports:[ConfigModule], providers:[IPFS_PROVIDER], exports:[IPFS_PROVIDER] }` would have zero inbound edges to the vault/ipfs cycle, so importing it from all three modules would not create or worsen any cycle. The actual cycle is `IpfsModule → VaultModule` (controller needs `VaultService`); a provider-only leaf module is orthogonal to it.

- Deeper fix: extract the leaf `IpfsProviderModule` and import it in all three places. ~15 lines net, removes the triplicated factory, and the factory becomes the single owner of the `LocalProvider` construction + default URLs.
- Why still only debt: it's a pure-DI dedup with no behavioral effect, and the factory bodies are currently identical, so nothing is broken today. Concrete cost: the comments enshrine an incorrect justification ("shared module → circular dep") that will mislead the next person into thinking the cycle is unbreakable, and the `LocalProvider` default-URL strings are now triplicated (a config-default change must hit three files). I'd capture this as a todo and fix the comment wording even if the extraction is deferred — the "accepted because circular" rationale is the part that's wrong, not just unpolished.

---

Clean / correctly-scoped (no findings): the CID-regex DTO bound (`unpin.dto.ts`), the `recordUnpin` deletion (IN-03 — dead code, right call), the `controller` rollback switching from `guardedUnpin` to direct `unpinFile` (WR-02 — correct: the row never existed so routing through `guardedUnpin` was the wrong layer; direct provider call is the right altitude), the `fileUnpins` metric gating (IN-01), and the WR-04 collect-concurrency limit.

Summary: file the (a)+(d) lock/recheck duplication and the (c) provider-module extraction as two todos. (c) additionally warrants a one-line comment correction now, since its stated rationale is factually wrong. Nothing here blocks the phase.

## Why both `completed/` and `done/` exist

They're the **same concept under two conventions that drifted**:

| Folder | Items | Status | Last git activity |
|--------|-------|--------|-------------------|
| `done/` | 70 | **legacy** | 2026-06-15 (frozen) |
| `completed/` | 20 | **current/canonical** | 2026-06-19 (today) |
| `pending/` | 15 | active | — |

The GSD tooling references `todos/completed` **6 times** and `todos/done` **0 times** — `close_phase_todos`, `check-todos`, `note`, and `add-todo` all read/write `completed/`. So around Jun 15 the convention switched from `done/` → `completed/` (likely a GSD update), and the 70 old `done/` items were never migrated. That's why I correctly put the phase-50 todos in `completed/`. It's drift, not intent — the clean state is a single resolved folder.

I'll ask whether to consolidate at the end. First, applying the /simplify results.

## /simplify synthesis

**Apply now (safe, in-scope, behavior-preserving — all in `client.ts`):**

# Phase 50 — Validation Strategy

> Per-phase validation contract for feedback sampling during execution.

---

## Test Infrastructure

| Property               | Value                                                                                     |
| ---------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Framework**          | jest 29.x (API plans 50-01, 50-02, 50-04, 50-05 — `apps/api`) + vitest 3.x (SDK plan 50-03 — `packages/sdk`) |
| **Config file**        | `apps/api/jest.config.js` (jest) · `packages/sdk/vitest.config.ts` (vitest)              |
| **Quick run command**  | API: `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api test -- --testPathPattern="vault.service.spec\|pending-unpin.processor.spec\|ipfs.controller.spec"` · SDK: `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk test` |
| **Full suite command** | `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api test` · `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk test`                |
| **Estimated runtime**  | API unit specs ~tens of seconds (~20–40s) · SDK unit ~seconds (~2–10s)                    |

Two frameworks are in play: the four API plans (50-01, 50-02, 50-04, 50-05) run under **jest** because they touch `apps/api`; plan 50-03 runs under **vitest** because it touches `packages/sdk`. The SDK vitest `include` glob matches only `src/**/*.test.ts`, so the new D-03 spec uses the `.test.ts` suffix.

---

## Sampling Rate

- **After every task commit:** Run the quick command for that task's file — API: `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api test -- --testPathPattern="vault.service.spec|pending-unpin.processor.spec|ipfs.controller.spec"` · SDK: `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk test`
- **After every plan wave:** Run the full suites — `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api test` and `pnpm --filter @cipherbox/sdk test`
- **Before `/gsd-verify-work`:** Both full suites must be green
- **Max feedback latency:** 60 seconds

Security review complete. Report written to `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/.planning/security/REVIEW-2026-06-19-phase50.md`.

## Summary

Counts: 0 CRITICAL, 0 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM, 4 LOW, 2 INFO (documented false-positives). Overall residual risk: LOW. This is a data-integrity/authorization phase, not new crypto, and the core model holds up.

Authorization model VERIFIED: `guardedUnpin` (`vault.service.ts:259-336`) checks per-user ownership, then a cross-user refcount under a per-CID advisory lock, and only queues a physical Kubo `pin/rm` when the global refcount hits zero. User A cannot drop content User B still pins. The public `/unpin` route is fully refcount-gated.

Top issues:

1. F1 (MEDIUM) — `LocalProvider.unpinFile` (`apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/local.provider.ts:87`) interpolates the CID into `pin/rm?arg=${cid}` with no `encodeURIComponent`. The `/unpin` route is mitigated by the new anchored DTO regex (`^(Qm...|b...)$`, MaxLength 255), but the function is unsafe by construction and has callers that bypass the DTO — the WR-02 compensation path (`ipfs.controller.ts:133`) and the drain processor. Not currently exploitable (those CIDs are server-derived or previously validated), but recommend a one-line `encodeURIComponent` fix at the source.

2. F2 (LOW/availability) — the Kubo `fetch` has no timeout, so a hung Kubo holds a DB connection (and, in the drain at `pending-unpin.processor.ts:86-109`, the per-CID advisory lock) across the network call for the full hang. `guardedUnpin`'s primary Kubo call is correctly outside the transaction; the drain holds the lock by design to prevent re-pin TOCTOU. Add an AbortController timeout.

3. F3/F5 (LOW) — `hashtext(cid)::bigint` collisions are benign (availability-only micro-contention; every locked section re-reads its own per-CID DB state keyed on the literal CID, so a collision can never act on the wrong CID's rows). The SDK cycle guard (`client.ts:303-368`) shares a `visited` Set across recursion and correctly terminates A→B→A cycles and diamonds; depth and `children` length are not capped (self-DoS only, fire-and-forget).

WR-02 path: scrutinized for cross-tenant abuse — the direct `unpinFile` only fires on a server-derived CID from the authenticated upload, cannot be fed an arbitrary CID, and its only refcount-bypass risk is the documented D-13 dedup race (cryptographically negligible given random per-file AES-256-GCM keys/IVs).

Crypto hygiene (F4/F6): ECIES `unwrapKey` throws a generic `CryptoError` with no key material (anti-oracle by design); the vault private key and unwrapped folder keys are never logged; traversal logs only public IPNS names and `err.message`. The unknown-CID vs cross-user paths return identical responses (no oracle). SQL in `backfill-pinned-cids.ts` is parameterized; the `INTERVAL '1 hour'` is a static literal, not user input.

Relevant files: `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipfs/providers/local.provider.ts`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/packages/sdk/src/client.ts`, `/Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box/scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts`.

```
This is the key finding to evaluate carefully. On `origin/main`, the rollback path called `guardedUnpin(userId, cid, {suppressCrossUserAudit: true})`. Crucially, in that prior path:

- `recordPin` had thrown, so there is no `pinned_cids` row for `(userId, cid)`.
- `guardedUnpin` does `findOne({where:{userId, cid}})` → returns null → **early `return`** (the "silent 2XX, no row" branch). It never reached the refcount/unpin step.

So on `origin/main`, the rollback was effectively a **no-op** when `recordPin` failed (the row was never written), meaning the orphaned Kubo pin was actually *leaked* (the bug WR-02 describes). The Phase 50 change replaces it with a direct `unpinFile(result.cid)` to actually remove the physical pin.

The concern: the new direct unpin skips the cross-user refcount check. If user A's upload deduped to a CID that user B already holds a live `pinned_cids` row for, and A's `recordPin` fails, the rollback now physically unpins B's live content. This is acknowledged in the D-13 comment as a sub-second race requiring identical ciphertext. Let me assess exploitability rigorously, since this is the only candidate touching cross-user impact.

The attacker (user A) would need to: (1) upload ciphertext byte-identical to victim B's already-stored file (requires knowing B's exact AES-256-GCM ciphertext including IV — B's content is encrypted client-side with B's keys, so A cannot produce identical ciphertext without already having it), AND (2) force their own `recordPin` to fail (e.g., quota — but quota is checked *before* pinFile at controller:103). Producing byte-identical ciphertext to another user's encrypted file is cryptographically infeasible in this zero-knowledge design (random IVs, per-user keys). So there is no attacker-controllable path to target a specific victim's CID.

I have completed the review.

## Security Review — Phase 50 (`feat/ipfs-ipns-data-integrity-fixes`)

No high-confidence (>= 8) newly-introduced, concretely-exploitable security vulnerabilities found.

I read full source context (not just hunks) for all in-scope non-generated/non-test/non-doc files: `apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts`, `apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts`, `apps/api/src/ipfs/dto/unpin.dto.ts`, the three `*.module.ts` IPFS_PROVIDER factories, `packages/sdk/src/client.ts` (the `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync`/unenroll dispatch area), and `scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts`.

What I checked and why each candidate was rejected:

1. Direct `unpinFile(result.cid)` rollback added at `apps/api/src/ipfs/ipfs.controller.ts:133` (replaces a `guardedUnpin(..., {suppressCrossUserAudit:true})` call). This is the only change touching cross-user physical-unpin and the most security-relevant. It bypasses the refcount check, so in theory it could physically unpin a deduped CID another user holds a live `pinned_cids` row for. Rejected as non-exploitable: (a) `result.cid` is server-derived from Kubo's `pinFile` response, never attacker-supplied; (b) to target a specific victim's CID an attacker would have to upload byte-identical AES-256-GCM ciphertext (including IV) to the victim's file — cryptographically infeasible under this zero-knowledge design (per-user keys, random IVs); (c) the prior `origin/main` path was actually a no-op here anyway (no `pinned_cids` row exists after `recordPin` throws, so the old `guardedUnpin` hit its early `return` and never unpinned), so this is a fix for a pin leak, not a new authz regression. The residual race is the self-documented sub-second D-13 window with no attacker-controllable trigger.

2. SQL injection in the new advisory-lock and refcount paths (`vault.service.ts:267,322`; `pending-unpin.processor.ts:89`). All use parameterized queries (`$1` / `[cid]`) and TypeORM query builders. No string interpolation of user input. The `hashtext($1)::bigint` change (dropping the buggy `abs()`) is a correctness fix, not a SQL surface change.

3. CID reaching `LocalProvider.unpinFile`'s unescaped `pin/rm?arg=${cid}` URL. The interpolation line is pre-existing (commit from phase 7, unchanged on this branch) and is a captured todo, not a Phase 50 change. Every CID reaching it is now either server-derived (Kubo `pinFile` response CIDs via the controller rollback and processor drain, which read `pending_unpins` rows that only `guardedUnpin` inserts) or validated at the route boundary by anchored regexes — `UnpinDto` (`@Matches(/^(Qm...|b...)$/)` + `@MaxLength(255)`, tightened in this phase) and `RegisterCidDto`. Phase 50 narrows this path rather than widening it.

4. SDK `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` recursion over client-supplied decrypted folder metadata (`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`). The new `visited` set guards cycles/diamonds; the only failure mode is client-side stack/fan-out exhaustion (excluded as DoS) and it is client-side anyway (backend is the trust boundary). The IPNS names it collects are dispatched to a server `ipnsControllerUnenrollBatch` endpoint whose authorization is unchanged and out of scope; no key material is logged (catch blocks log only the IPNS name/count).

5. `scripts/backfill-pinned-cids.ts` changes (WR-05 age cutoff, WR-06 real `is_byo_user` projection). Static SQL string with no interpolated input; it is a trusted operator CLI tool, and the changes make deletion selection stricter. No injection or new authz surface.

No hardcoded secrets, no crypto misuse (ECIES `unwrapKey` failures are caught and never logged with key material), no deserialization/code-exec, and no sensitive-data exposure in logs/errors were introduced by these changes.

28	# Phase 50: Code Review Report
29	
30	**Reviewed:** 2026-06-19T00:00:00Z
31	**Depth:** deep
32	**Files Reviewed:** 13
33	**Status:** issues_found
34	
35	## Summary
36	
37	Reviewed the IPFS/IPNS data-integrity fixes: the advisory-lock overflow fix,
38	refcount-aware drain, quota-decrement-on-delete in `guardedUnpin`, CID
39	validation DTOs, the backfill script, and the new on-demand subtree IPNS
40	collector in the SDK client.
41	
42	The advisory-lock fix, the in-transaction quota decrement, the drain refcount
43	re-check, and the backfill safety guards are sound and well-tested. However the
44	new on-demand traversal (`collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync`) drops the cycle guard
45	that the analogous `ensureFolderLoaded` DFS carries, exposing an unbounded-
46	recursion / stack-overflow path on malicious or corrupt folder metadata — and
47	every caller dispatches it as a `.then()` with **no `.catch()`**, so that
48	failure surfaces as an unhandled promise rejection. There is also an unguarded
49	TOCTOU window in the drain worker's refcount re-check. CID-validation regexes
50	are inconsistent across DTOs. Details below.
51	
52	## Critical Issues
53	
54	### CR-01: On-demand subtree collector has no cycle/visited guard — unbounded recursion
55	
56	**File:** `packages/sdk/src/client.ts:272-325`
57	**Issue:** `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` recurses into every `folder`-typed
58	child but, unlike `ensureFolderLoaded` (line 549, which keeps a
59	`visited = new Set<string>()` "guard against repeats and pathological cycles"),
60	it carries **no visited set and no depth bound**. Folder metadata is
61	client-supplied, decrypted, attacker-or-corruption-influenceable data. If
62	folder A's metadata lists folder B and B's metadata lists A (a cycle), or a
63	deep/wide adversarial tree is crafted, the traversal recurses without
64	termination. The per-child `catch` at line 317 does **not** break the cycle:
```

Resolves the Phase 42 unpin-integrity findings (an INT_MIN-hash CID stays deletable; a re-pinned CID is never drained) and makes folder deletion unenroll **every** descendant IPNS record even when the subtree was never loaded in the session.\n\n**Status:** Verified — 3/3 goal must-haves confirmed in code.\n\nThis closes two long-standing data-integrity bugs: (1) the guarded-unpin advisory lock could permanently 500 on one specific CID, leaking quota and making the file undeletable; the pending-unpin drain could physically remove a re-pinned (live) CID, causing Kubo GC and data loss; and (2) `collectSubtreeIpnsNames` only walked already-loaded folder state, so deleting an unexpanded folder left nested IPNS records republishing forever.\n\n## Changes\n\n### Wave 1 (parallel)\n\n- **50-01 — advisory-lock overflow** (`vault.service.ts`): drop `abs()` from `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($1)::bigint)`. `abs(INT_MIN)` overflowed, making that CID permanently undeletable. RED→GREEN.\n- **50-02 — refcount-aware drain** (`pending-unpin.processor.ts`): re-check `pinned_cids` count before physical unpin; skip the unpin and drop the stale outbox row when the CID is re-pinned. RED→GREEN.\n- **50-03 — on-demand subtree IPNS collection** (`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`): new `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` fetch+decrypts persisted child folder metadata on a `folderTree` miss (never mutating `folderTree`, to avoid the Zustand/SDK desync). RED→GREEN.\n- **50-05 — D-04 dispositions** (controller / `UnpinDto` / backfill / modules): WR-02 physical unpin on the no-row compensation path, IN-02 CID format+length validation, WR-05/WR-06 backfill query safety, IN-04 accepted module-cycle comments; api-client regenerated.\n\n### Wave 2\n\n- **50-04 — quota + metric dispositions** (`vault.service.ts`, `pending-unpin.processor.ts`, `docs/CAPACITY.md`): IN-01 gate `fileUnpins` on a confirmed row delete, IN-06 rename `outboxRowInserted` → `shouldAttemptPhysicalUnpin`, IN-03 remove dead `recordUnpin`, WR-04/WR-07/IN-05 accepted dispositions.\n\n### Post-review hardening (code review found and fixed)\n\n- **CR-01** add a `visited` cycle guard to `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` (an A→B→A folder-metadata cycle would otherwise stack-overflow on attacker/corruption-influenceable data).\n- **CR-02** add `.catch()` to the fire-and-forget unenroll dispatch sites (no more unhandled promise rejection).\n- **WR-01/WR-03** serialize the drain recheck+unpin and the guarded-unpin outbox-row delete under the per-CID advisory lock.\n- **WR-04** bound the unenroll traversal fan-out with `pLimit`.\n\n### Quality\n\n- `/simplify` extracted two shared helpers in `client.ts` (bin-unenroll dispatch, folder-subtree collect) — behavior-preserving.\n\n## Requirements Addressed\n\n- **HARD-01** — IPFS/IPNS data integrity (no data loss, no permanently-undeletable CIDs).\n\n## Verification\n\n- [x] Goal verification: 3/3 must-haves confirmed against the codebase (`50-VERIFICATION.md`).\n- [x] Nyquist validation: compliant, 0 gaps (`50-VALIDATION.md`).\n- [x] Unit tests: api `vault.service` / `pending-unpin.processor` / `ipfs.controller` 90/90; sdk `collect-subtree-ipns-names` 4/4 (incl. an A→B→A cycle-termination test).\n- [x] Build/typecheck: full package chain + web `tsc -b` clean.\n- [x] Code review: blockers + in-scope warnings fixed (`50-REVIEW.md` resolution table).\n- [x] Security: crypto pass + adversarial branch pass — 0 exploitable findings; authorization model (ownership row → cross-user refcount under advisory lock → Kubo `pin/rm` only at refcount 0) verified sound (`.planning/security/`).\n\n## Key Decisions\n\n- The on-demand traversal never writes fetched metadata back into `folderTree` (keeps it local), avoiding the documented stale-sequence 409 / resurrected-delete desync.\n- The drain holds the advisory lock across the Kubo unpin to close the re-pin TOCTOU window (deliberate; low-frequency batched path — recorded as a known availability caveat).\n- `pg_advisory_xact_lock` accepts signed bigint, so the `abs()` was both unnecessary and the sole failure mode.\n\n## Follow-ups (captured todos, out of this phase's scope)\n\n- `register-cid-dto-validation-inconsistency` (WR-02 regex divergence — recommend a shared `CID_REGEX`/`@IsCid()`).\n- `local-provider-unescaped-cid-in-pin-url` (WR-05 — `encodeURIComponent`; both security passes confirmed non-exploitable today).\n- `extract-withcidlock-shared-unpin-primitive` and `extract-leaf-ipfs-provider-module` (de-duplicate the advisory-lock SQL / `IPFS_PROVIDER` factory).\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n\necho \"body bytes: $(wc -c < \"$PR_BODY_FILE\")\"\necho \"=== bare #NN refs in body (should be none) ===\"\ngrep -oE '(^|[^`])#[0-9]+' \"$PR_BODY_FILE\" || echo \"(none — clean)\"\necho \"\"\necho \"=== create PR ===\"\nenv -u GITHUB_TOKEN gh pr create \\\n  --title \"fix: IPFS/IPNS data-integrity fixes for unpin and folder unenroll\" \\\n  --body-file \"$PR_BODY_FILE\" \\\n  --base main 2>&1 | tail -8\nrm -f \"$PR_BODY_FILE\"",

}

  "timeout": 1600000,
  "run_in_background": true
}

FILE: apps/api/src/vault/vault.service.ts:283
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟡 Minor_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Gate `rowDeleted` on actual delete result.**

At Line 281, `rowDeleted` is set to `true` unconditionally after `delete()`. If `delete` affects 0 rows, `fileUnpins.inc()` still fires, which undermines the IN-01 metric guarantee.

<details>
<summary>Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
-      await pinnedCidRepo.delete({ userId, cid });
-      rowDeleted = true; // IN-01: row confirmed deleted; gate the metric below
+      const deleteResult = await pinnedCidRepo.delete({ userId, cid });
+      rowDeleted = (deleteResult.affected ?? 0) > 0; // IN-01: only true on actual deletion
```
</details>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR hardens IPFS/IPNS data integrity across three axes: the advisory-lock overflow that permanently blocked one specific CID from being deleted, a TOCTOU drain that could physically unpin a re-pinned live CID (data loss), and folder deletion that skipped IPNS unenrollment for subtrees never loaded in-session.

- **50-01/50-04** (`vault.service.ts`): drops `abs()` from the advisory-lock hash cast (INT_MIN overflow → undeletable CID); renames `outboxRowInserted` → `shouldAttemptPhysicalUnpin`; gates `fileUnpins` metric on a confirmed row delete; adds a second serialized transaction for the post-Kubo outbox-row delete (WR-03).
- **50-02** (`pending-unpin.processor.ts`): `drainRow` wraps the refcount re-check + Kubo unpin under the same per-CID advisory lock used by `guardedUnpin`, closing the TOCTOU re-pin window; also properly drops stale outbox rows when a CID is found to be re-pinned.
- **50-03** (`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`): `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync` now falls back to on-demand IPNS fetch+decrypt when a folder subtree is absent from `folderTree`, with a cycle guard, per-child error isolation, and `pLimit` concurrency bound; all three call sites updated with `.catch()` on the fire-and-forget chain.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

The three core data-integrity fixes are correctly implemented and safe to merge.

Two minor issues found: dead code in the catch block of `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync`, and a JSDoc that overstates advisory-lock

<a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> The `if (!visited.has(entry.ipnsName))` guard in the catch block is always true and can never fire. The outer `if (visited.has(entry.ipnsName)) continue` at line 349 already ensures we only reach the try/catch when the entry is absent from `visited`, and neither `unwrapKey` nor any sequential code between the two checks modifies `visited`. FILE apps/api/src/ipfs/pending-unpin/pending-unpin.processor.ts:84
<a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=9" align="top"></a> The JSDoc claims this method serializes "against concurrent **recordPin**/guardedUnpin for the same CID," but `recordPin` never acquires `pg_advisory_xact_lock`, so a concurrent re-upload's `recordPin` is not blocked while `drainRow` holds the lock. The window the comment targets — drain sees `refs=0`, a racing `recordPin` inserts a fresh `pinned_cids` row, drain's Kubo `unpinFile` then removes the live pin — technically remains open. In practice it requires an identical CID collision (same AES-GCM ciphertext + IV) which is cryptographically negligible and tracked by D-13, but the claim "closes that window" is imprecise. The advisory lock only serializes against concurrent `guardedUnpin`; the `recordPin` gap is covered by D-13/drift detection, not the lock.
---

Looking at `collectSubtreeIpnsNamesAsync`: the recursive call at line 357 mutates `visited` **and** `acc` at its own entry (lines 314-316) *before* it can throw (e.g., the for-of at 343 throws on malformed/corrupt `children` — exactly the threat model). Greptile's analysis missed the recursive mutation — **finding 1 is a false positive; the guard is load-bearing.** I'll keep it and reply.

> AGENT

Read it — **Greptile finding 2 is valid and important.** The JSDoc (lines 74-83) claims the advisory lock "closes that window," but the WR-01 race is drain↔`recordPin`, and `recordPin` never takes the lock — so the lock actually only serializes drain↔`guardedUnpin`. The residual drain↔`recordPin` sub-window is covered by **D-13** (colliding on the same CID needs byte-identical ciphertext+IV — cryptographically negligible), not the lock. I'll correct the comment to say that precisely.
